#### Reply 18

**Author:** Randy_Warner
**Posted:** 2025-11-28

to evista0120 Hey evista0120​! I think this is a great goal. Unfortunately there aren't any "off the shelf" solutions yet. The core concept that you are referring to here will need to use a method called Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG). In short, this is where your question would be used to retrieve specific, relevant data from your business database, and then that data would be provided to the LLM (OpenAI/ChatGPT) as context for generating the final answer. This is totally possible to achieve with a few solutions however the initial investment wouldn't be a small one. My guess is that you would regularly be asking the same questions. In my experience a solid dashboard with the KPIs that matter to you and dynamic user inputs for things dates, users/crews, etc. could deliver the same information for a fraction of the cost. Back to your original point though, I've often thought it would be fun to create an Alexa app to connect to your database for a similar experience as you have described although I'm not sure what the overall demand would be for a solution such as this.

## 49. 🚨FEATURE REQUEST: Tiered Pricing on Products & Services 🚨

**Author:** seanroz (Contributor 4)
**Posted:** 2025-07-28
**Views:** 378 | **Likes:** 7 | **Comments:** 8
**URL:** https://community.getjobber.com/discussions/operations-forum/%F0%9F%9A%A8feature-request-tiered-pricing-on-products--services-%F0%9F%9A%A8/4908

### Content

Hey Jobber Team and Fellow Pros, Let’s talk about a feature that could seriously boost close rates and make Jobber even more competitive for all of us who quote services, manage inventory, and work in price-sensitive markets. What we need: Tiered Pricing on Products and Services — customizable pricing where the unit cost automatically adjusts based on quantity ordered. Why this matters: We already price materials mulch, sod, and stone this way in real life. It would speed up quoting, improve estimate accuracy, and help us win more jobs. It mirrors how customers expect to see pricing — more they buy, less they pay per unit. How it would work: Let users define pricing tiers for any product/service: 1–10 units = $10/unit 11–50 units = $8/unit 51+ units = $6/unit These price breaks should auto-calculate during estimate creation and carry through to invoicing. Why Jobber Should Care: Makes Jobber more competitive vs. other platforms offering advanced pricing features. Helps your users convert more jobs = more usage and more loyalty to Jobber. Reflects real-world pricing logic we already use outside the app. If you'd use this — drop a comment or to help get this in front of Jobber’s dev team. Let’s get this done together! AI AUTO DETAILING CLIENT COMMUNICATIONS CONSTRUCTION CONTRACTS & CONTRACTING CUSTOMER SERVICE & EXPERIENCE GENERAL CONTRACTING INSTALLATION SERVICES INVOICING IRRIGATION SERVICES LANDSCAPING LAWN CARE PROPERTY MAINTENANCE QUOTING REPORTING & BUSINESS INSIGHTS SCHEDULING SERVICE LISTS SOFTWARE VISIT REMINDERS

to AswathVeersoft If you're a field service business using GetJobber, you've probably noticed that while it's excellent for scheduling and invoicing, the email notification system is pretty basic. You can send appointment reminders and job follow-ups, but that's about it. No sophisticated workflows, no conditional logic, no multi-step campaigns, and definitely no way to automatically chase down those overdue invoices at 2 AM while you sleep. That's why we created NotifyScout – a powerful email notification engine that plugs directly into GetJobber and gives you the kind of automation that enterprise platforms Zoho CRM, Hubspot and Customer.io provide, but purpose-built for field service businesses. Here's what NotifyScout Plan: Smart Payment Notifications: Get instant alerts when payments come in, when invoices go overdue, or when a high-value client's balance crosses your comfort threshold. Your bookkeeper gets notified 3 days before an invoice is due, and if the client hasn't even opened the invoice after 3 days, you'll know to follow up. Time-Based Workflows: Set up sophisticated email sequences "Send estimate → Wait 3 days → Send 'Any questions?' follow-up → Wait 4 days → Send limited-time discount → Wait 1 week → Send final reminder." Perfect for converting those abandoned quotes into booked jobs. Field-Change Triggers: Automatically send a thank-you email the moment a job status changes to "Completed," or trigger a review request when an invoice gets marked as "Paid." Your team doesn't lift a finger – it all happens automatically. Conditional Logic: Send different emails based on customer type, job value, or service history. Commercial clients get one message, residential another. First-time customers get a welcome series, VIP clients get white-glove treatment. High-value jobs (over $5K) automatically notify the owner for review before sending. Real Analytics: Track open rates, click rates, and email engagement for every notification. See which emails are working, which aren't, and optimize accordingly. Built-in A/B testing lets you compare subject lines and content to maximize response rates. Multi-Step Sequences: Build entire customer journey campaigns – from the initial "Welcome to our service family" email when a new client signs up, through quarterly maintenance reminders, to "We miss you" win-back campaigns for clients who haven't booked in 12 months. The best part? NotifyScout receives data from GetJobber via webhooks in real-time, enriches it using GetJobber's GraphQL API, and stores everything in a flexible format that lets you build any notification rule you can imagine. We're not trying to replace GetJobber – we're making it 10X more powerful. Visit http://www.notifyscout.com Why visit now instead of waiting? ## 51. Why I Stopped Chasing Jobs and Started Building Systems

**Author:** ryaantuttle (Jobber Ambassador)
**Posted:** 2025-10-28
**Views:** 117 | **Likes:** 2 | **Comments:** 1
**URL:** https://community.getjobber.com/discussions/operations-forum/why-i-stopped-chasing-jobs-and-started-building-systems/7401

### Content

Early in my career I equated motion with progress. AKA: Rocking chair syndrome. If the phone rang, I was winning. If my schedule was full, I was successful. But all that movement was just noise. I was busy, not effective. The shift came when I started documenting everything! How calls were answered, how quotes were written and how materials were ordered. Once I built systems, my business stopped depending on how many hours I worked and started depending on how well I led. Systems create predictability. Predictability builds trust. Trust builds freedom. Stop being the technician who holds it all together with duct tape. Be the leader who builds a machine that runs smoothly, even when you step away. Have questions? Message me! CONSTRUCTION CUSTOMER SERVICE & EXPERIENCE GENERAL CONTRACTING HANDYMAN REPORTING & BUSINESS INSIGHTS SERVICES LISTS

### Replies (1)

#### Reply 1

**Author:** julie
**Posted:** 2025-11-28

JOBBER COMMUNITY TEAM Hey Ryan, love how you framed the difference between being busy and being effective. That “rocking chair syndrome” line is such a visual way to put it. I can see how documenting and building systems you described can totally change the way a business operates. It’s such a great reminder that growth often comes from structure, not just hustle!

## 53. Is everyone calculating commission in a spreadsheet?

**Author:** Daichi (Contributor 4)
**Posted:** 2025-09-28
**Views:** 252 | **Likes:** 2 | **Comments:** 7
**URL:** https://community.getjobber.com/discussions/operations-forum/is-everyone-calculating-commission-in-a-spreadsheet/6730

### Content

I have a landscaping business. I have a pay for performance model, pay out for google reviews, and am thinking about providing sales people with a commission for specific types of jobs. Right now, I use a whiteboard to show the crew and calculate in Excel. Is there a better way to do this? I feel paying a flat rate (percentage of invoice or flat rate per sale) is simple. But when it comes to tracking hours, or paying based on margins, upsells etc. It can get pretty complicated tracking everything in Excel. LANDSCAPING LAWN CARE REPORTING & BUSINESS INSIGHTS SOFTWARE

### Replies (7)

#### Reply 1

**Author:** MatthewL
**Posted:** 2025-10-28

This is our Review board. $25 per review paid out at the end of the quarter. About $2500 by the end of quarter 3. Quarter four we have this plus the “door hanger” contest. Pass out the hangers to neighbors of jobs. Write your name on it. CSR will ask the caller whose name is on it. $1000 to the winner at our quarter 4 mtg the week before Christmas. For commission we give our sales guy a decent weekly salary plus 2% commission on all jobs. He keeps track of the jobs completed and turns it in each week and we just double check it. We specialize in fence and decks so he earns an extra $100 for each of these sold. Works well for us. If a technician gets an upsell they also get 2%. If anyone brings us a job that was not our original Lead such as a friend or family member etc. we give them 25% of the profit for that job.

## 58. What’s your best “DIY hack” you’ve used to run your business?

**Author:** ryaantuttle (Jobber Ambassador)
**Posted:** 2025-08-28
**Views:** 125 | **Likes:** 2 | **Comments:** 1
**URL:** https://community.getjobber.com/discussions/operations-forum/what%E2%80%99s-your-best-%E2%80%9Cdiy-hack%E2%80%9D-you%E2%80%99ve-used-to-run-your-business/5670

### Content

When I first started my business over 19 years ago, estimates meant pen, paper, and carbon copies—rip the top page for the customer, keep the yellow one for myself. That was just the way it was back then, and it worked. Trips to Staples were the norm! But as I think about it, over the years I’ve also come up with plenty of little “DIY hacks” to keep things running when resources were tight or when I didn’t have the systems I do now. Some of those scrappy fixes actually held up surprisingly well! I bet most of you have similar stories— What’s the best “DIY hack” you’ve used to run your business? Maybe it was how you scheduled jobs, tracked expenses, did marketing, or just stayed organized before you had tools Jobber. Sometimes those old-school solutions are just as clever as the technology we use today. Can’t wait to hear yours! ## 59. How Are You Hiring? Anyone Using AI in the Process?

**Author:** judithvirag (Builder 1)
**Posted:** 2025-08-28
**Views:** 87 | **Likes:** 2 | **Comments:** 2
**URL:** https://community.getjobber.com/discussions/operations-forum/how-are-you-hiring-anyone-using-ai-in-the-process/5694

### Content

BUILDER 1 Hiring is one of the biggest challenges for service businesses ours. I'm curious how others in the industry are approaching it today — especially with all the new tools out there. A few questions for the group: Where are you finding your best applicants (Indeed? Facebook? Referrals?) What does your interview or onboarding process look ? Is anyone using AI tools to assist with hiring? For example: Auto-screening resumes AI chatbots for pre-interviews Video interview analysis tools Auto-scheduling interviews Predictive scoring or assessment tools I'm always trying to stay ahead and improve the way we bring people into our company, so I’d love to hear what’s working for you — or what tools you’re considering. Thanks in advance for sharing! AI

### Replies (2)

#### Reply 1

**Author:** ryaantuttle
**Posted:** 2025-08-28

This is the one system we keep pretty basic since we tend to "recruit" all year instead of searching when the flames are getting too hot. Whenever we run ad's for team members, we always split-test that ad to see which one is performing better. From there we set up a quick "discovery" call to get a feel for the candidate and see if WE want to take the next steps together. Next steps: we send them an email with all of our requirements to work together. If they respond back with what we requested, we meet them in-person at one of their jobs or ours. After that, we either give them their first job or run. If they get the first job, we continue working together until we have customers that are not happy. We've done this successfully for the last 5 years with some team members still with us from day one.

#### Reply 2

**Author:** judithvirag
**Posted:** 2025-08-28

BUILDER 1 We also have a one week training period and this is a great time to determine if they are a fit. We also recruit all year along and I am working on automating my hiring process up to the In Peson Interview. Super exciting.

Hey Everyone MIT just dropped a study that tracked 54 participants using ChatGPT, Google, or no tools at all while completing writing tasks. They ran EEG scans to measure brain engagement. So what does this mean for us ... in home services lol. 🔻 Cognitive Drop: Brain activity was lowest in ChatGPT users ❌ Low Ownership: Users couldn’t explain or recall their own work 📉 Underperformance: LLM users got worse over 4 months ⚠️ Backfire Effect: People who started with AI then went to just thinking normally actually performed worse than those who never used AI. A lot of people are “faking it” better now with AI. But in home service and contracting, you can’t fake real understanding, hard skills, and service. If you don’t know your pricing, if you can’t explain your value, if you haven’t built systems or have your own vision, then ChatGPT isn’t helping you grow. #### Reply 45

**Author:** MrElectro
**Posted:** 2025-08-28

to MERTSPRODUCTS I’ve been experimenting a lot with n8n and Jobber recently and, honestly, the workflows themselves aren’t the hardest part — it’s getting a stable connection to Jobber in the first place. Their OAuth flow is a bit unique, and token refresh handling is what trips most people up. Without that, even the best workflow will stop running after a while. Zapier avoids the issue because they’ve got a native integration, but Make.com and n8n doesn’t — so you’re left with webhooks or rolling your own OAuth app. Then there’s the stuff you don’t see in demo videos: historical data pulls that hit API limits, retry logic for flaky endpoints, rate limits, and idempotency checks so you don’t accidentally duplicate records. Getting something to run once is easy — getting it to run every day without breaking is the challenge. Ai is great I use just about all of them the only is it can loose context quickly or hit and error and start digger a deeper hole if your not careful. I finally got a setup working in n8n that handles Jobber OAuth, refresh tokens, webhooks, and reliable historical pulls, and from there I’ve been building plug-and-play automations for common Jobber tasks. If anyone’s stuck on the connection part or just curious about what’s possible once it’s stable, I’m happy to share a high-level overview or show some examples of the kinds of workflows I’ve been testing.

## 61. How do you measure crew productivity?

**Author:** Daichi (Contributor 4)
**Posted:** 2025-08-28
**Views:** 178 | **Likes:** 3 | **Comments:** 5
**URL:** https://community.getjobber.com/discussions/operations-forum/how-do-you-measure-crew-productivity/5460

### Content

I know Jobber has the employee productivity report. It doesn't work great for a company that has a ton of recurring jobs with fixed billing. I'm curious how people are measuring their crew performance. There are so many ways to track it. Budgeted hours vs actual, revenue per hour worked, jobs completed, visits completed - the list goes on. Curious to know what works for you and your team. What metrics do you look at to gauge crew productivity? CLEANING ELECTRICAL CONTRACTING LANDSCAPING LAWN CARE PLUMBING PRESSURE WASHING REPORTING & BUSINESS INSIGHTS SOFTWARE

### Replies (5)

#### Reply 1

**Author:** Conrad
**Posted:** 2025-08-28

A really quick & simple check I know some people use is weekly revenue per employee (or crew) divided by weekly hours worked. For example $5,870 / 38hrs =$154.47/hr. That allows simple comparison between employees/crews. (Relax I’m in Australia that’s not USD. 😂) From there you will hopefully have already dug deeper into back costing to establish a GP% target, but it’s really GP$/hr that will let you drill down into your profitability, which is a function of productivity and pricing. Every job should be back costed, you can’t manage what you don’t measure. We also do majority (~74%) recurring work, for the moment we use the Jobber profitability calculation as our quick check. I know it needs to be around 80-85%. If it slips, we need to check why. If it was a once off, that’s ok (rain delay, had to pause work for pedestrians etc.) but if it’s happening constantly and the crew are working efficiently the job just needs a price increase, it’s often as simple as that. I would bet most businesses don’t increase their prices as often or as much as they should…

#### Reply 2

**Author:** Daichi
**Posted:** 2025-08-28

to Conrad Appreciate the in depth message. You said you use Jobber's profitability calculation as a quick check. If it is below that 80% mark. What does diving into that job look ? You're looking at visit time sheet data and seeing if it's consistent?

#### Reply 3

**Author:** Conrad
**Posted:** 2025-08-28

to Daichi Most of our work is straight labour, no materials. So the profitability % is a simple calculation of labour cost vs invoiced amount. I have found that if you end up invoicing extras that aren't included on the job it will skew the profitability % as it calculates based on the job price not the invoice amount. But it's still ok for a quick check. If the % dropped to say 70% then yes I'd look at the job time/labour cost and dig deeper to see what happened/what went wrong. I would still back cost manually every so often as this will give you more historical data to see patterns etc. Jobber profitability % is useful for a quick "running check" but you need to take the reigns and dig deeper into your numbers to really be sure things are heading the right direction in the long term.

#### Reply 4

**Author:** judithvirag
**Posted:** 2025-08-28

BUILDER 1 We take the hours they are allowed and actually how much they spend on the job. The goal is if they have a 4 hour job to be done in 3.75 or less. This gives them their productivity percentage.

## 68. What is your favorite time-saving app?

**Author:** RobsonGrant (Contributor 2)
**Posted:** 2025-07-28
**Views:** 121 | **Likes:** 0 | **Comments:** 1
**URL:** https://community.getjobber.com/discussions/operations-forum/what-is-your-favorite-time-saving-app/4955

### Content

Hands down, my favorite productivity tool for Windows is Beeftext (sorry, Mac users!). As an executive administrative assistant and customer service representative, this app saves me countless hours of typing every week. What is Beeftext? Beeftext is a free, open-source text substitution tool for Windows that transforms short keywords into longer text snippets. Whether you’re tired of retyping email signatures, markdown templates, or your favorite kaomojis, Beeftext lets you create “combos” that automatically expand your shortcuts in any app that supports copy and paste. It’s lightweight, respects your privacy, and is completely ad-free—ideal for anyone who types a lot and wants to work smarter, not harder. You can learn more and download it here: https://beeftext.org/ The developer confirms it’s 100% safe and clean: https://github.com/xmichelo/Beeftext/wiki/Beeftext-is-safe Getting started is simple: https://github.com/xmichelo/Beeftext/wiki/Getting-started Pro tip: I use the equals sign (=) as my trigger key since I rarely need it otherwise. For example, typing =hi automatically expands to: Hi, it’s Rob from Wright Restorations Canada. Thank you for reaching out. How can I assist you today? Beeftext is very user-friendly, works in most apps and only takes a few minutes to master. Highly recommended for anyone looking to save time and reduce repetitive typing! CLIENT COMMUNICATIONS CUSTOMER SERVICE & EXPERIENCE SOFTWARE

**Author:** julie (Jobber Community Team)
**Posted:** 2025-02-28
**Views:** 15 | **Likes:** 5 | **Comments:** 17
**URL:** https://community.getjobber.com/discussions/operations-forum/how-do-you-handle-scheduling-conflicts-or-last-minute-changes-in-your-business/155

### Content

JOBBER COMMUNITY TEAM It happens! But when it does, do you have a process in place to handle these situations? CLIENT COMMUNICATIONS CUSTOMER SERVICE & EXPERIENCE SCHEDULING

### Replies (17)

#### Reply 1

**Author:** ryaantuttle
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

Yes! Systems and processes for everything. It's also best to have this in an SOP or Employee Handbook, too.

#### Reply 2

**Author:** julie
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

JOBBER COMMUNITY TEAM to ryaantuttle Hard yes to an SOP or Employee Handbook 👏

#### Reply 3

**Author:** sandiashineco
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

I just text or call them depending on the situation. If their service is still 3+ days out, I'll text them. If it's less than that, I'll call, leave a VM then text. Depending on who the client is and if it's going to really mess things up for them, I may give them a discount or an upgrade to their service for free. I try to lean towards finding a super inexpensive service I offer as a bonus before giving a discount. Honesty and taking responsibility for the occurrence I think is very important in this kind of situation.

#### Reply 7

**Author:** Conrad
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

Majority of our work is regular lawn services (lawn mowing). So we make sure that we'll have access to the property if no one is home, and operate a floating schedule so we can move visits as needed. This helps to work around rain delays and other uncontrollable delays. And also helps make it easier to deal with staff sick days, equipment damage/repairs etc. It just comes back to communication with the customer, we basically let them know that we'll manage the scheduled and keep things on track, and that we aim to keep visits scheduled on the same weekday, but that may be adjusted during the season.

**URL:** https://community.getjobber.com/discussions/operations-forum/jobber-ais-confusion-with-businesses/4382

### Content

I switched my AI receptionist service from Goodcall to Jobber's AI. One thing I noticed is that when a business calls in, it asks for the customers name only. When a business name gets involved, it will either use the business name as the customers last name or not take note of the company name at all. This leads to some big-time confusion. Is there a fix for this or has anyone else experienced this? AI CLIENT COMMUNICATIONS SCHEDULING

## 73. Best way to handle inbound calls to company line?

**Author:** Tdog1212 (Contributor 2)
**Posted:** 2025-02-28
**Views:** 12 | **Likes:** 5 | **Comments:** 14
**URL:** https://community.getjobber.com/discussions/operations-forum/best-way-to-handle-inbound-calls-to-company-line/1749

### Content

Curious yalls thoughts. Looking to not just grow, looking to scale and improve / continue to implement systems. Currently have myself, 1 outside sales rep, and field labor crew (fence install company) current process: customer calls into company # (my cell phone). I try to answer as if it were an office line to answer asap. From that, I confirm I can Text them, I then send a request form via jobber that has basic info / few questions to answer. If / when they fill it out, I add to the schedule for a confirmed day / time to quote on site. etc…… I feel this part is a lot of back and forth, and until I have an in house admin office worker that can answer these calls the first ring - I won’t be able to truly grow / stay efficient. (If I’m tied up, I don’t calling them back 2 hours later, etc) but also - I love having them fill out the form bc the way I have questions on it, it turns it from a warm lead, to a warmer lead. Any way to streamline this, get more efficient, improve this current process? ANY thoughts or advice - real thankful. AI CLIENT COMMUNICATIONS CUSTOMER SERVICE & EXPERIENCE FENCE SERVICES QUOTING SOFTWARE

### Replies (14)

#### Reply 1

**Author:** KellyGuerrero
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

Hire a Virtual Assistant (VA). A VA can be a game-changer, especially when you're not ready for a full-time in-house admin. We’ve been using a VA through Upwork for three years, and she handles inbound calls, schedules quotes, and takes down all the job details. This allows us to provide immediate assistance, keeping potential clients engaged and turning warm leads into HOT leads because everything is handled on the spot. Here’s how it works: The VA answers the calls right away, acting as the first point of contact. She collects all the information about the job and schedules the appointment for our sales rep to go out. By taking this off your plate, it frees you up to focus on higher-level tasks and ensures no lead falls through the cracks. Plus, VAs can do more than just answer calls—they can handle customer emails, follow-ups, and other admin tasks, making them a super cost-effective solution as you scale. Ours is now in a full-time role and she tracks our company KPIs, is in charge of billing and collections, as well as most customer service functions.

#### Reply 2

**Author:** Tdog1212
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

to KellyGuerrero Super helpful. is she paid per call handled or what does the comp setup look ?

#### Reply 3

**Author:** KellyGuerrero
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

to Tdog1212 She's paid through upwork and it's hourly. However, she's in the Phillipines so it's a much lower per hour cost than what we would get here in the US. Right now I'm paying $10/hour but keep in mind we may be a much larger operation than what you have. We have four field crews, 1 sales person, and two admins. One is state-side and one is overseas. There are also services that specialize in this type of data entry for Jobber that do charge per call. Before we were with Jobber, we had utilized one of these services first and they were OK, but it was different. It was more a process of, "we'll get the request information input and then someone has to followup with them". I'd recommend going for a VA because it frees up much of your time, and empowers your sales person to do what they do best and you focus on what you do best instead of all the back and forth work. Overall, I strongly believe that it will help you deliver a better customer experience.

#### Reply 17

**Author:** CourtneeEzra
**Posted:** 2025-06-28

to Conrad Thank you! It's been going pretty well but always looking at improving systems and increasing profits! We have built a an Ai scoring system based of many variables such as estimated job value, complexity, job variables etc Some of the rules: Online Quote: Suitable for simple, standardized jobs with clear requirements, low complexity, and costs under £300. Video Quote: Best for medium complexity jobs needing visual assessment in the cost range of £200-£800. Site Visit: Appropriate for complex jobs or high-value work, such as structural or electrical jobs, costing over £500. We analyse: Job Complexity Information Completeness (dimensions, materials, access) Need for Visual Assessment Risk Factors (scope creep or complications) Value Range (estimated total cost) Customer Urgency This framework allows us to choose the highest-scoring method based on job characteristics. Take a look at this landing page with the "Get a free estimate" button and give it a try! https://www.handymannottingham.club (optimised for mobile traffic only) Your feedback would be greatly appreciated as this version is still in beta 😊

### Content

As someone who runs a handyman business remotely from Vietnam, I’m always looking for ways to save time and make things more efficient—especially around quoting. Quoting small jobs used to be a major drain. Driving out just to quote something “can you fix a door” would often result in no-shows, delays, or jobs that weren’t even worth taking. So I built an AI-powered tool that now handles this automatically. Here’s how it works: A customer fills out a short form online The AI analyzes the job description Based on that, it either: Sends a WhatsApp message asking for a video Gives an instant rough quote Or recommends a full in-person visit and books it It’s been a game-changer for our workflow. No more guessing. No more wasted time. No more back-and-forth on simple jobs. I’d love to get your thoughts on it — especially if you run a service-based business. Here’s the link to try it out or give feedback: Handy Services App What do you think? Could this save you time too?

### Content

For years, estimating was the one thing I couldn’t take off my plate. We changed the org chart. We hired roles. Delegated everything we could. But estimating? That was always me. Even if I wasn’t doing anything else in the business... I was still stuck quoting jobs. It was the bottleneck I couldn’t fix—until now. I built a ChatGPT-powered estimator trained with my systems, my pricing, and my language. It asks the right questions, runs the math, and delivers estimates I would—without me being involved. Now I’m no longer the bottleneck. Customers get quick answers. I get my evenings and weekends back. Want to build your own? Map out your estimating logic. Plug it into ChatGPT. Test and refine. If you're stuck working all day and doing estimates at night and on Saturdays anddddd, sometimes even Sunday mornings when everyone's sleeping—this might be your way out. Arka offers a cloud-based solution focused on cost and inventory management, making it easier to track stock levels, manage purchases, and analyze costs. #### Reply 7

**Author:** LukeSalmonArm
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

to kyle-rottman Hi Kyle-Rottman, Thanks for the . Good thing that you have an Excel wizard to work some magic for you. We use Excel quite a bit too, but for inventory, I wanted something a little more user-friendly and 'live' so that all of our sales people could see real-time inventory data with pricing and other info. Lightspeed worked decently for us, but it is set up more for strictly retail businesses (ie. clothing store). We sell a small amount of our parts inventory to DIY'ers or other contractors who come into our store, but most of our inventory gets used for install or service work that our crews are doing, so Lightspeed wasn't a perfect fit either. What I liked about it most was the functionality around physical inventory counting. We could create partial count lists and complete them easily using a cell phone as a mobile scanner. That way we could bite off small pieces of an overall inventory count into manageable tasks without missing something or having to dedicate a large chunk of time to counting everything at once. The problem started when they decided that they were more interested in being a credit card processing company rather than an inventory management company. We didn't use the built-in invoicing capability of Lightspeed since inventory sales is such a small part of our business, so using them for credit card processing didn't make sense for us. And, I really didn't the $270 CAD monthly 'convenience fee' that showed up unexpectedly on our account because we didn't use their credit card processing services! For now, I've just switched over to using QuickBooks for inventory management. It is nowhere near as convenient, and physical counts are going to be a nightmare, but at least it allows for live inventory management and pricing.

#### Reply 18

**Author:** CoDuctDoctors
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

to ryaantuttle Example would be Bonusly. Employer can provide awards, incentives for performance..We use Bonusly. Basically you can create any type of award or incentive for customers completing a job quicker, or cross-selling other services while at the jobsite to an existing client.. For example, we incentive our technicians for speed and accuracy, positive customer-feedback (google reviews) that mention the techs name, cross-selling other services, and attendance record quarterly. With Bonusly, you create a point system and what those points are worth. We have our techs keep track of the what they cross-sell in order to get incentive and recognized for it, they log into Bonusly at the end of the day and enter the recognition name #cross-sell and required to upload the workorder and the system dumps the points into their wallet for the specific amount of points they earn for cross-selling. For every 10 points can equal $ 1, 3, or 5 dollars. We get to decide how much 10 points is worth. So 500 points is $50 they can deem this is set up initially when you set up the account. You can set up one off or quarterly performance bonuses which is why we this software. They add up, techs can save up and redeem Visa debit gift cards, cards to over 100 stores. We don't pay until the employee redeems the reward. If the technician has $50-$500 dollars worth of points they turn them into cash redeeming virtual or physical gift cards, then we get charged once they redeem. Our accountant says this considered a "fringe benefit".

#### Reply 40

**Author:** CoDuctDoctors
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

We just started using Bonusly.. It doesn't integrate with Jobber but it does with Quickbooks. You can control awards, incentives.. Its $3/ per employee per month and we select the pay-as-redeemed- option. Basically you can create any type of award or incentive. We incentive our technicians for speed and accuracy, positive customer-feedback (google reviews) that mention the techs name, cross-selling other services, and attendance record quarterly. With Bonusly, you create a point system and what those points are worth. We have our techs keep track of the what they cross-sell in order to get incentive and recognized for it, they log into Bonusly at the end of the day and enter the recognition name #cross-sell and required to upload the workorder and the system dumps the points into their wallet for the specific amount of points they earn for cross-selling. For every 10 points can equal $ 1, 3, or 5 dollars. We get to decide how much 10 points is worth. So 500 points is $50 they can deem this is set up initially when you set up the account. You can set up one off or quarterly performance bonuses which is why we this software. They add up, techs can save up and redeem Visa debit gift cards, cards to over 100 stores. We don't pay until the employee redeems the reward. If the technician has $50-$500 dollars worth of points they turn them into cash redeeming virtual or physical gift cards, then we get charged once they redeem. Our accountant says this is a "fringe benefit". Meaning we can reward the employee in cash value without having to pay payroll taxes and neither does the employee. It's a true 1 for 1 payout, and it's a standard operating expense under an employee benefit/perk. If you go to Bonuslys website, they push their middle tier option which includes peer-to peer appreciation at $5/month and $60 per subscriber per year, which is more expensive and as a service provider nothing our team cares to use. So you have to specifically ask for the "pay as redeemed option" and no peer-to peer feature to eliminate the $60 year per subscriber, and also lowers the employee cost monthly from $5 to $3.

## 84. Clocking in/out

**Author:** NatalieG81 (Contributor 2)
**Posted:** 2025-02-28
**Views:** 720 | **Likes:** 1 | **Comments:** 7
**URL:** https://community.getjobber.com/discussions/operations-forum/clocking-inout/1445

### Content

Hi everyone! My company is new to Jobber and we are working on getting all the details set up within the portal. So far so good, but there is one hurdle we are trying to overcome - technicians' capability to clock in and out for the day. I do not want it to be possible for them to do so on their phone because, as everyone can imagine, time theft could occur. Though I understand that there are features for automatic location timers and way points, it seems that would be more work involved for us to monitor accuracy and make edits on time punches on a daily basis. I also know that we can set up a tablet in the office for our techs to clock in/out when they are physically on site to start and end their day, but using their email to log into Jobber to do so would pose the issues - most of our techs are not English speaking and do not use email so it would take time for them to log in/out each day. Our previous program had the capability for our techs to clock in/out using a tablet in office with a 4-digit code (the last digits of their phone number). We liked this because of the ease, speed, and simplicity it took for everyone. Sorry for my long-winded post, but all in all, I'm wondering if anyone has any suggestions on ways we can make the daily clocking in/out the most efficient and uncomplicated for EVERYONE involved, meaning techs AND office personnel. I appreciate the help and advice! REPORTING & BUSINESS INSIGHTS

### Replies (7)

#### Reply 1

**Author:** FCHSDinah
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

The automatic location feature is the only option I can recommend, but I'm unaware of whether the technicians can use that data to support reconciling their hours at each site at the end of the day?

**Author:** TheProcessCEO
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

While it takes time to implement, my recommendation is to utilize jobbers clock in and out features. We have 28 employees and they clock in and out for the day using jobber, and they clock in and out of each job. The gps is marked for each transaction and we did have to monitor them closely for about 6 months until they all realized we were not messing around. we have now been using it for 4 years and it’s amazing. We take that timesheet report and enter it into adp for payroll. It’s really helped us streamline our process. it’s hard implementing something new, but it is necessary to grow. I hope this helps - you can do it!

#### Reply 7

**Author:** Roger
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

Depending on your Jobber plan, you can create individual logins for your technicians, which will separate each worker’s timesheets. Keep in mind that personal clock-in time is different from tracking how long a job takes to complete. While Jobber provides tools to monitor this, it’s still important to have someone verify that the work is actually being done. Regarding email setup, if your company uses a domain email service Google Workspace, you can create a shared email such as workers@mycompany.com and use aliases worker1@mycompany.com for each technician. This allows you to manage multiple accounts without incurring extra costs for additional email addresses. Jobber can easily set up new users under these email aliases, and you’ll have control over actions password resets. Workers don’t need full email access—just credentials to log in to the app. Ideally, you should provide a company device for this purpose to maintain control over your data and ensure it stays within your organization. I hope this helps! Feel free to reach out if you need further assistance.

ntroduction to OhioSupremeClean – How to Grow a Fully Automated Cleaning Business Dear Community, I hope this message finds you well. We focus on quality, reliability, and efficiency, ensuring our clients enjoy spotless spaces without the hassle. As I work on expanding my business, I’m looking for insights on hiring employees, competitive pay rates, and the best way to transition from a solo operation to a fully automated cleaning system—one that can run efficiently without my constant presence. My goal is to build a streamlined business that generates $200,000+ per year, leveraging AI tools and technology to automate scheduling, customer service, marketing, and operations. I have a few key questions: Hiring the First Employees – What’s the best way to attract and hire reliable cleaning staff? Should I start with contractors or full-time employees? Competitive Pay Rates – What is a fair and competitive starting wage for cleaners in Columbus, Ohio? Scaling Up & Automating – With today’s AI tools, is it realistic to build a self-sustaining cleaning business? What technologies or systems would you recommend to automate operations? Growing to $200K+ Annually – What are the key steps to reach this revenue milestone while ensuring quality and efficiency? ### Replies (1)

#### Reply 1

**Author:** judithvirag
**Posted:** 2025-03-28

BUILDER 1 Hi Ahmendoor, Congrats on growing and expanding. Here are my comments to your quesitons, hope it will help and spark some ideas. 1. Start with employees instead of contractors to ensure quality control and loyalty. Use a structured hiring process that includes reference checks, training, and clear expectations from day one. Develop core values, mission and vision and hire based on this. 2. Pay rates vary by location, but a good starting point is slightly above market rate to attract and retain talent. Research local wages and consider adding incentives referral bonuses or performance-based raises. Consider making offers with total compensation in mind. 3. Yes, AI tools can help automate scheduling, customer service, and marketing. Jobber is amazing for scheduling, automated text/email follow-ups, and AI-driven customer inquiries. 4. Focus on securing recurring clients, optimizing efficiency with checklists and training, and leveraging technology to streamline operations. Expanding into condos or partnerships with property managers can accelerate growth while maintaining quality. Focusing on Google My Business - getting good reviews and SEO. Thanks, Judith

**Author:** HotTubDave (Contributor 3)

### Content

When we first started up, I signed up for a virtual phone number through a service Calle Vumber.com. It's worked fine for 9 years. It suddenly occurred to me that I am totally **bleep** if Vumber.com decides to cease operations tomorrow. I assume this could be a similar issue for anyone using a phone number from one of the hundreds of virtual calling companies in the market. My options are: 1) continue as is and hope nothing goes wrong. 2) Port my phone number to Verizon, who charges almost 12 times as much (times 3 phone numbers) and have to deal with the lousy Verizon service, but at least know that my number will always be there. Has anyone ever given this any thought? Has anyone ever lost a number due to their virtual phone service shutting down?

any other service, phone lines can face interruptions. ## 94. The Importance of Proper Planning in New Construction and Home Improvement Projects

**Author:** NewcombLand (Contributor 2)
**Posted:** 2025-02-28
**Views:** 134 | **Likes:** 1 | **Comments:** 1
**URL:** https://community.getjobber.com/discussions/operations-forum/the-importance-of-proper-planning-in-new-construction-and-home-improvement-proje/1382

### Content

Embarking on a new construction or home improvement project can be an exciting venture, but without proper planning, it can quickly become a costly and time-consuming ordeal. One of the most critical aspects of this planning involves understanding the timelines and requirements associated with land surveying and permit acquisition. Plan Ahead to Avoid Delays In the land surveying industry, turnaround times for documents can vary significantly depending on the time of year and current demand. It is essential to plan ahead and allow for a lead time of anywhere from 4 to 8 weeks for surveying and the creation of permit or plot plans. This time frame ensures that all necessary details are accurately captured and processed. Understand the Approval Process For projects that require recorded maps, such as those needing county approval and recording, the process can take even longer. Typically, this approval and recording can take a minimum of 2 to 3 months. Being aware of these timelines can help you plan your project more effectively and avoid unexpected delays. Get Accurate Quotes and Timelines When seeking quotes for land surveying services, it is crucial to obtain detailed turnaround times and to ask when the final documents will be ready for delivery. Some surveyors may promise quick fieldwork, but the actual mapping and documentation might take significantly longer. Ensure you are comparing apples to apples when evaluating quotes from different providers. This means understanding not just the cost, but also the scope of work and the time required to complete all necessary tasks. By properly planning your new construction or home improvement project, you can avoid unnecessary costs and wasted time. Being aware of the various stages and timelines involved in land surveying and permit acquisition allows you to make informed decisions and keep your project on track. Remember, the key to a successful project is not just in starting strong but also in ensuring every step is meticulously planned and executed. Feel free to reach out if you have any questions about the land surveying process. CONSTRUCTION CONTRACTS & CONTRACTING CUSTOMER SERVICE & EXPERIENCE FENCE SERVICES GENERAL CONTRACTING HANDYMAN HVAC LANDSCAPING POOL & SPA REMODELING SCHEDULING

#### Reply 1

## 95. What would you like to see added/changed in Jobber to make your life easier?

**Author:** Essential_RV (Contributor 2)
**Posted:** 2025-02-28
**Views:** 274 | **Likes:** 2 | **Comments:** 4
**URL:** https://community.getjobber.com/discussions/operations-forum/what-would-you-like-to-see-addedchanged-in-jobber-to-make-your-life-easier/1324

### Content

I own and run a one-man RV inspection and mobile repair business in North Carolina. I've been using Jobber for 2 years and it's been the best FSM I've used so far but it's not perfect. Here are some things I'd to see added/changed in Jobber to make my life easier. On a job that has multiple operations, I'd to be able to lump all the parts and labor for a single operation together. This is commonly done at automotive shops and gives the customer a view of what each operation costs. In NC, if a part that's installed is taxable, so is the labor to install it. For this reason, I must have separate labor services, one for non-taxable and one for taxable. This gets to be tedious when you take a call from a client not knowing what you will get into and use the non-taxable service by default only to discover after being on the job that you need to use parts that are taxable and then you have to change the labor type. I'd to see a Taxable check box next to each item on the invoice that can be marked or changed as needed. I enter my products and classify them as taxable or not and this doesn't usually change. It's the labor that can change. I would the ability to customize my dashboard with the information that's important to me. For instance, I'd to add Products and Services to the dashboard for easier access since I'm in there all the time. QBO does this. A-la-carte feature pricing. I hate paying for things I don't use. The feature packages are getting expensive and the next level has features I'll never use but has one or two that are very good. Let me pick and choose the features I want and price the package accordingly. These are a few things that have been bugging me. What features would you to see added/changed?

### Replies (4)

#### Reply 1

**Author:** nesto
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

Our business requires a site visit to get the project scope and provide a quote. Being able to have some type of ability to use voice to put together a quote on the drive back to the office would save sooo much time and get quotes to clients quicker

#### Reply 2

**Posted:** 2025-02-28

to nesto You can easily use AI to do estimates this and make a zap for them toto be completed I believe. Also, you can template most estimates out and create "Similar Quotes" and/or "Jobs" to send within seconds.

to ryaantuttle Controlled by voice though? I’m talking about a button you just press in the app and audibly say what actions you want to happen ”create quote for X client, add X product with quantity of X”

#### Reply 4

**Author:** ryaantuttle
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

That sounds a bit fancy. As you know, ALL softwares have limitations, although that is ALL going to be changing very soon. Companies that can't keep up will be left in the dust with all of the AI changes currently being implemented. They will pull further and further away at a rapid pace. That's on the reasons I love Jobber so much. They've done a great job advancing new features while still keeping an eye on the basics.

## 98. Quote Disclaimers - General Contracting

**Author:** mioh (Contributor 2)
**Posted:** 2025-02-28
**Views:** 602 | **Likes:** 1 | **Comments:** 3
**URL:** https://community.getjobber.com/discussions/operations-forum/quote-disclaimers---general-contracting/1133

### Content

What kind of disclaimers, contract language do you include in every quote you send? We are a general contractor so the disclaimers can be vague-ish compared to a roofing company, etc. I just had a situation where I needed to add a dust control disclaimer. I asked ChatGPT for 5 disclaimers and it was super helpful but I'm curious what you always list on your quotes. For reference, this is what ChatGPT recommended: Here are five contract disclaimers for a general contracting business: Unforeseen Conditions: While we strive to accurately assess all project details during the planning phase, unforeseen conditions (such as hidden structural damage, hazardous materials, or utility conflicts) may arise during the project. These conditions could impact the scope, timeline, or cost of the project and will be addressed through a change order. Material Delays: [Your Company Name] is not responsible for delays caused by the unavailability of materials, supplier shortages, or other supply chain issues beyond our control. Any resulting delays may affect the project timeline, and adjustments will be communicated as promptly as possible. Owner-Provided Information: [Your Company Name] relies on the accuracy of information, plans, and documentation provided by the client or their representatives. Errors, omissions, or inaccuracies in this information may result in additional costs or delays, which are the responsibility of the client. Third-Party Contractors: In instances where the client employs third-party contractors or suppliers not under our direct control, [Your Company Name] assumes no liability for their performance, workmanship, or delays. Coordination with these third parties may impact the overall project schedule or quality. Environmental Factors: [Your Company Name] is not responsible for delays, damage, or additional costs caused by weather, natural disasters, or other environmental factors beyond our control. Any necessary adjustments to the project scope or timeline due to such events will be documented and discussed with the client. Each disclaimer ensures clarity in client expectations and protects your business from potential disputes. CLIENT COMMUNICATIONS GENERAL CONTRACTING QUOTING

### Replies (3)

#### Reply 1

**Author:** ryaantuttle
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

This is always a fun topic and one of the most serious, if you ask me. This is where you want to consult a lawyer once you've outlined your contract/proposal agreement. Each state is different and the laws are not always applicable. Writing up these proposals is a way you can protect yourself, team , family and business. So, looking for and building a relationship with a lawyer is critical. And, there's all types of lawyers, too. We have 1 for litigation, 1 for corporate and 1 for trademarks. It's an entire team that communicates with our CPS, too, so our **bleep** ARE COVERED! I see too many contractors working their **bleep** off, only to argue about getting paid , change orders or having to do work they NEVER agreed, too. We have an entire folder of FREE Forms, Template and contacts for downloads in our mentoring business group for contractors. You can find it here, along with a FREE booklist. https://www.skool.com/home-masters-mentoring/about

#### Reply 2

**Author:** NateHardy
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

to ryaantuttle In addition, I always add a “Rock” clause. With new builds you never know what you may run into with excavation. Putting in utilities etc… In northern Arizona Malapai can be the bane of any excavators existence.

#### Reply 3

**Author:** JanineChambers
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

If you are a contractor I’m assuming you work on large projects, hopefully you have an external document that your customers sign prior to work starting. I typically add a service in Jobbers products and services that references the external document and/or general terms of service hosted on the company website. If you are still building your formal contract document I would reference one that you have signed when hired by another contractor in your area/field. If it is a large company they have likely paid attorneys to review and fine tune the verbiage to legally protect them. yours may not need to be as in depth but you can change the wording to what you do and offer.

I think this could be a SIMPLE fix, by making the right connection. It sounds you'd be much better off staying with Jobber and making some Zapier connections to Asana and/or something Airtable. Im not a huge proponent of switching softwares if there's just some small limitations that can be easily fixed. It's much too costly and not necessary if the options exist. Go on Upwork and hire a freelance "Developer" that can log into your Jobber and Zapier account. They'll be able to handle the rest from there. Simplicity is always key

We also have a job board so we can see where all our jobs are in the production cycle as well.

## 106. 🌟 Ask An Expert: Practical Uses for AI with Bob Evans 🌟

**Author:** julie (Jobber Community Team)
**Posted:** 2025-02-28
**Views:** 722 | **Likes:** 3 | **Comments:** 6
**URL:** https://community.getjobber.com/discussions/operations-forum/%F0%9F%8C%9F-ask-an-expert-practical-uses-for-ai-with-bob-evans-%F0%9F%8C%9F/611

### Content

JOBBER COMMUNITY TEAM AI is a buzzword, but how are other business owners actually using it day-to-day? From Oct 9 - 15, Bob Evans will be answering all your AI questions, sharing practical ways to use AI from automating tasks to boosting customer satisfaction. Don't miss out—drop your questions below and tune in for the answers! Who's Bob? 🤔 Bob is a Principal Product Manager at Jobber specializing in AI and Automations. If you missed his appearance at our Jobber Blueprint: Live event "Multiply Your Time: Practical, Day-to-Day Uses for AI" check out upcoming events here: jobber.com/events. AI CLIENT COMMUNICATIONS CUSTOMER SERVICE & EXPERIENCE REPORTING & BUSINESS INSIGHTS SOFTWARE

### Replies (6)

#### Reply 1

**Author:** julie
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

JOBBER COMMUNITY TEAM I’ll be sharing a few questions throughout the week that were asked in our Jobber Blueprint: Live event 😎 How customizable are Jobber AI features How much work does it take to customize AI for your business?

#### Reply 2

**Author:** Bob_Evans
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

to julie Hey Julie! Great questions. We are constantly working to add more abilities for service pros to tailor AI features within Jobber to perform the way that suits them best. Right now we are working on ways to allow customization to Jobber AI Receptionist to allow service providers to indicate tone of voice, and have more methods for how to train Jobber AI Receptionist on how to handle calls and inquiries. In general, the more feedback you provide to AI tools about the responses they generate, the faster it will improve and understand your preferences! But it's tough to provide a direct answer on how long it would take to customize AI for a business - that depends on just how custom you need to be.

#### Reply 3

**Author:** julie
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

JOBBER COMMUNITY TEAM Hey Bob! Here is our second question from one of our webinar attendees: How will Jobber AI Receptionist handle complex or emotionally sensitive customer interactions?

#### Reply 4

**Author:** Bob_Evans
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

to julie It's great that people are thinking about this. You will have control over how Jobber AI Receptionist interacts with your customers. You can direct it to answer questions in a certain way, or recommend certain actions, based on the information provided by the customer in the call. You'll also have access to logs of text and phone conversations between AI Receptionist and your customers, so you can make sure things are being handled to your standards and make tweaks as needed. I'm looking forward to the next question!

#### Reply 5

**Author:** sandiashineco
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

Hey Bob, what is an AI tool most of us aren't aware of but we should be looking into as a 1-2 person service business? I do all of my business functions and have part time tech with me. I leverage Jobber to help keep things aligned and flowing without loosing my mind. I tend to use Google Gemini to help work though concepts or ad copy or even SOPs quickly. I've seen a marketing friend use some AI tool that he trained on one of his clients, how he writes, what the mission of an ad campaign is, etc. and could use it in ways to create emails and even more. I don't feel Chat GPT or Gemini get that nuanced.

#### Reply 6

**Author:** Bob_Evans
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

to sandiashineco Hey sandiashineco, Just to call this out, since you’re already using ChatGPT, is their recent release of Canvas. It’s a tool that allows you to collaborate with ChatGPT to build a document/content together. You can provide it guidance on why you’re drafting it, underlying details that it should consider, and then things length & tone. If you find that the back-and-forth of the chat interface isn’t really helpful for drafting content this is the tool for you.

## 107. What's your method for flagging important client notes?

**Author:** wesleyparker (Contributor 3)
**Posted:** 2025-02-28
**Views:** 314 | **Likes:** 2 | **Comments:** 1
**URL:** https://community.getjobber.com/discussions/operations-forum/whats-your-method-for-flagging-important-client-notes/472

### Content

Hey power users: I'm sharing our method and wondering if anyone has a better system for important client notes. We deal with hundreds of client accounts every day and the notes are not visible "above the fold." This leads to important notes being missed often. We started adding an emoji #️⃣ to the client name as a visual cue to the office that they should scroll down and read the notes on this account. It's clunky and the emoji gets dropped during QBO sync sometimes so we have to use both the # and #️⃣. See screenshot below. What do you do? Anything cleaner? SCHEDULING SOFTWARE

### Replies (1)

#### Reply 1

**Author:** seanroz
**Posted:** 2025-02-28

Great topic! Here is what we currently do to communicate important notes on recurring jobs. For Important Job Notes: If it's a recurring visit, we edit job instructions to read "See notes 📝" Attach the note to the client profile, while linking to job notes Pin the note on the client's profile to the top so it stays on top for service teams Keeping the verbiage consistent in the job instructions builds a healthy habit for our teams always to check the notes. Adding the "See notes" in the job instructions allows for easy visibility of the list schedule view within the jobber mobile app.
